INSURANCE DEPARTMENT OF ARIZONA Forty-Fourth Annual Report of the Director of Insurance of the State of Arizona 

TO: 

The Honorable Members of the 

Legislature of the State of Arizona 

Sirs: 

Pursuant to the provisions of law, I hereby transmit this, the Forty- 

Fourth Annual Report of the Director of Insurance as of December 31, 1956. 

Statistics shown in this annual report are on a calendar year basis, the latest year ending December 31, 1956. 

Statistics relating to Insurance Department receipts and expenditures are on a fiscal year basis with the latest fiscal year ending June 30, 1957. 

Respectfully submitted,

G. A. BUSHNELL 

August 20, 1957

Director of Insurance

PRINCIPAL ACCOMPLISHMENTS OF THE INSURANCE DEPARTMENT IN 1956 

Collected more than $2,300,000 in premium taxes, fees and licenses for the State Treasurer. 

Administered the provisions of the Arizona Insurance Code of 1955 Passed upon qualifications of insurers applying for admission to do business in Arizona. 

Examined the financial statements and premium tax returns of all insurers doing business in Arizona and the affairs, transactions, accounts, records and assets of domestic insurers. 

Inspected and filed about 5,000 affidavits for surplus line placements. 

Reviewed about 9,000 policies and about 7,500 fire and casualty rate filings submitted for approval for use in Arizona.

Investigated and serviced about 2,500 policyholder's complaints and answered numerous inquiries from the general public.

Processed about 50, 000 insurer's appointments of agents and about 10,500 agent's, broker's, solicitor's and miscellaneous licenses.

Prepared, supervised and graded about 2, 700 written examinations of applicants for agent's, broker's and solicitor's licenses.

Responded to the steady inflow of correspondence, questionnaires and requests pertaining to insurance.

LIQUIDATION OF ARIZONA INSURANCE COMPANIES Twenty (20) Year Period Ending July 31, 1957 

Over a period of twenty (20) years this department has been called upon to act as Receiver for the purpose of liquidating only four (4) domestic insurance companies which failed in the interim. All four (4) of these companies failed because of the lack of prudent management and were dissolved as insolvent by Order of the Court.

UNION RESERVE LIFE INSURANCE COMPANY 

Dissolved March 4, 1938

Under date of March 4, 1938, the Court directed the Division of Insurance of the Arizona Corporation Commission to liquidate the business and affairs of this company. The assets of this company consisted mostly of mortgages of grossly inflated value, and on August 20, 1945, the Receiver filed a final report with the Court. 

TRANS-PACIFIC INSURANCE COMPANY 

Dissolved September 7, 1955

Dissolution notices have been sent to policyholders, creditors and other parties at interest. The further progress of this liquidation depends upon the adjudication of litigations in various States for the purpose of marshaling and collection of assets. 

NATIONAL SECURITY LIFE INSURANCE COMPANY Dissolved November 8, 1956 

Under date of November 8, 1956 National Security Life Insurance Company, a limited capital stock company, was dissolved as insolvent by Order of the Superior Court of the State of Arizona. 

Pursuant to the

Order, notice of dissolution was mailed to all policyholders and creditors. No stockholders assessment was necessary to provide funds for the payment of approved claims since the administration of the receivership was successful, and on May 8, 1957, all claims approved by the Court were paid one hundred cents on the dollar. 

INSURED SAVINGS INSURANCE COMPANY Dissolved November 30, 1956 

Under date of November 30, 1956, Insured Savings Insurance Company, a benefit insurance corporation, was dissolved as insolvent by Order of the Superior Court of the State of Arizona. Pursuant to the Order, notices to all policyholders and creditors were mailed. On August 16, 1957, approved claims against this estate were paid ninety cents on the dollar.

OPINIONS OF THE DEPARTMENT OF LAW OF CONTINUING IMPORTANCE RELATING TO THE TRANSACTION AND REGULATION OF INSURANCE 

September 20, 1956

Opinion No. 56-144 

August, 1956 - July, 1957 

Robert Morrison, Attorney General

The Department was advised that a nationwide nonprofit corporation (which, in consideration of an annual prepayment of a $1.00 fee, provides members with whole blood for transfusions through a blood bank clearing house) is neither an insurer within the meaning of A. R. S. Section 20-104 because the corporation agrees to provide a service rather than to pay an indemnity nor a hospital or medical service corporation within the meaning of A. R. S. Section 20-822 because the services to be provided are not pursuant to contracts with any hospital or physician. 

January 17, 1957

Opinion No. 57-9 

The Department was advised that an omnibus clause in a corporation's articles of incorporation, authorizing it "to do all things incidental or advantageous" in furtherance of named objectives and purposes, would not of itself enable the corporation to qualify as an insurance agent or broker within the meaning of A. R. S. Section 20-295 because the Constitution of Arizona, Article 14, Section 4 limits the business of a corporation to that "expressly authorized in its charter". 

March 5, 1957

Opinion No. 57-39 

The Department was advised that an insurer entitled to a refund for overpayment of retaliatory taxes could not secure satisfaction simply by deducting the returnable amount from a tax liability subsequently accruing but had as its only remedy the procedure of administrative adjustment set forth in A. R. S. Section 

35-191(D) because no other provision for refund is prescribed by law. 

June 6, 1957

Letter Opinion 

The Department was advised that a lending institution which causes individual life or disability coverage under an issued group debtor policy to be extended to customers and which collects premiums from them (rather than assuming same as an operational expense) acts in the manner defined by A. R. S. Sections 20-106, 20-282, 20-283, 20-286 and 20-287 and must, therefore, itself be licensed as an insurance agent, broker or solicitor and must in each place of business maintain in its behalf an individual licensed as insurance agent, broker or solicitor. 

June 20, 1957

Opinion No. 57-89 

The Department was advised that public employees, who may not qualify for "employee" group life or disability coverage because the State by Constitution, Article 9, Section 1 would be prohibited from contributing towards payment of premiums in the manner specified as prerequisite for group "employee" coverage under A. R. S. Section 20-1252, might nevertheless qualify for group coverage as a "labor union" under A. R.S. 

Section 20-1254, if organized as an association having as its purposes bargaining with the employer about terms and conditions of employment and improvement of economic and social status of members. 

July 8, 1957

Opinion No. 57-99 

The Department was advised that members of a non-profit corporation, consisting of persons engaged in the practice of medicine, could not qualify for group life coverage under A. R.S. Section 20-1251-B-2 (which authorizes coverage for 'Individuals" who own a business or who maintain active managerial and substantial legal or equitable interest therein) first because said law by its very language contemplates coverage only for specific individuals rather than for unspecified members of a broad class or group and secondly because the members are neither owners of the corporation nor actively engaged in its management. 

July 17, 1957

Opinion No. 57-103 

The Department was advised that, in the absence of a departmental ruling to the contrary duly promulgated under A. R. S. Section 20-143, a domestic benefit life insurer, immediately upon receipt of annual premiums paid in advance, could allocate same to mortuary and expense funds and would not be required by A. R. S. Section 20-943A to hold such advance premiums in trust for future allocation pending arrival of succeeding anniversary due dates. 10
